## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region (the BLACK-floored strip with BLUE walls) each step.
- Robots need to find and stay in the BLACK-floored strip near BLUE walls.
- Key perceptions: floor color (BLACK = target zone), blue color (BLUE walls = target direction).

**Champion Analysis:**
The champion (score 12436.29) uses:
- State 0: Spiral (speed 9) - exploration
- State 1: Exploration (rwm=41) - random walk
- State 2: GoToColor (blue=3) - move toward blue walls
- State 3: Attraction (att=2.866) - aggregate with neighbors

The champion moves toward blue (color 3) which are the walls of the target strip, and aggregates. This makes sense.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Exploit: small tweaks to champion topology/parameters
3. Explore: new topologies focused on getting to BLACK floor (target) and staying there

Key insight: Robots should go to BLACK floor (C=0 triggers leaving), or go toward BLUE walls (B=8, clr=3). Once on BLACK floor, stop or aggregate.

Let me design machines:

**Exploitation variants of champion:**
- Tweak spiral speed, attraction gain, GoToColor behavior
- Adjust transition probabilities

**Exploration ideas:**
- 2-state: GoToBlue → if on black, aggregate/stop
- 3-state: Explore → GoToBlue → Aggregate on black
- Use floor=BLACK (C=0) as "arrived" signal → stop/attract
- Use floor=GRAY (C=1) as "not arrived" → go toward blue

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 4216.69):**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) → if BLACK floor (p=0.8) go to State 1, else random (p=0.005) go to State 2
- State 1: Attraction(3.0) → if GRAY floor (p=0.3) go to State 2, if neighbors>3 (steep) go to State 2, if neighbors<1 (steep) go to State 0
- State 2: Exploration(rwm=50) → if sees blue (p=0.7) go to State 0, if on BLACK floor (p=0.5) go to State 1
- State 3: Stop → if BLACK floor (p=0.9) go to State 1, if GRAY floor (p=0.3) go to State 0

Logic: Go toward blue walls → arrive at black floor → aggregate. If lost, explore until seeing blue again. State 3 (Stop) seems underused since nothing transitions into it.

**Key insight:** The champion works because GoToBlue finds the target strip, then Attraction keeps robots there. The exploration state handles recovery.

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Tweak attraction gain (3.0 → 2.5-4.0)
- Tweak transition probabilities (p=0.8 for black floor detection)
- Adjust exploration random walk rate
- Make state 3 (Stop) reachable or replace with something useful

**Exploration strategy:**
- Try connecting state 3 properly (add transition from state 1 to state 3)
- Try FlockCenter instead of Attraction
- Try adding a 4th useful state
- Try simpler 2-3 state machines

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 3835.62):**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) → if BLACK floor (p=0.9) go back to State 0 (self-loop?), wait - with 2 states, `--n0x0 0` means destination index 0 among OTHER states, which is State 1. Similarly `--n0x1 0` also goes to State 1.
- Actually with 2 states, leaving state 0, the only other state is state 1, so D=0 always means state 1.
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) → if BLACK floor (p=0.9) → State 1; if random (p=0.003) → State 1
- State 1: Attraction(3.5) → if GRAY floor (p=0.3) → State 0; if neighbors<1 steep → State 0; if neighbors>3 steep → State 0

So: Go toward blue walls → arrive at black strip → aggregate. If lost (gray floor, no neighbors, or too many neighbors), go back to blue.

**Key insight:** This simple 2-state machine works very well. The BLACK floor detection (p=0.9) is the trigger to switch from navigation to aggregation.

**Exploitation:**
- Tweak att gain (3.5 → 3.0-4.5)
- Tweak transition probabilities (p=0.9, p=0.3, neighbor thresholds)
- Tweak neighbor condition parameters (w values)

**Exploration:**
- Add a 3rd state for recovery/exploration
- Try FlockCenter instead of Attraction
- Try different neighbor thresholds
- Try adding perception of blue walls while attracting

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Situation:** The champion hasn't improved since round 4 (score 3045.55). The score seems stuck. We need stronger exploration while maintaining elitism.

**Champion Analysis:**
- State 0: GoToBlue → if BLACK floor (p=0.9) → State 1; if random (p=0.003) → State 1
- State 1: Attraction(3.5) → if GRAY (p=0.3) → State 0; if <1 neighbor (w=10) → State 0; if >3 neighbors (w=5) → State 0; if sees blue (p=0.5) → State 0

**Why might this be stuck?**
- The "sees blue → go to blue" escape from aggregation state might be counterproductive (robots leaving the black strip when they see blue walls nearby)
- Attraction gain might not be optimal
- The >3 neighbors condition might be breaking up clusters

**New ideas to try:**
1. Remove the "sees blue" escape from aggregation - let robots stay once they arrive
2. Try higher attraction gains (robots need to clump tightly)
3. Try FlockCenter which might keep robots more cohesive
4. Try different neighbor thresholds (>5 instead of >3)
5. Try a stop state on black floor instead of attraction
6. Try GoAwayColor(green=2) to push robots away from green walls toward blue
7. Try combining GoToBlue with GoAwayGreen

# ROUND 7/10

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Situation:** Champion stuck at 3045.55 for 3 rounds. Need stronger exploration to escape local optimum.

**Why might we be stuck?**
- The 2-state GoToBlue→Attract machine is good but might not be optimal
- The "sees blue → leave attraction" escape condition (p=0.5) might be hurting - robots in the black strip near blue walls keep leaving
- The neighbor conditions in the attraction state might be breaking up good clusters
- Maybe robots need to aggregate MORE strongly once on black floor

**New directions to explore:**
1. **Remove "sees blue" escape** - once on black floor, stay and attract strongly
2. **Try GoAwayGreen(2)** as complementary navigation - push away from green walls
3. **Use floor=BLACK as a STAY signal** rather than a one-time trigger - add self-loop via very low random probability to leave
4. **Try Stop on black floor** - robots that arrive simply stop, reducing disruption
5. **Try attraction with NO escape conditions** - robots stay forever once they arrive
6. **Try combining blue-approach AND green-avoidance** for better navigation
7. **Use higher neighbor thresholds** - >5 instead of >3 to avoid breaking large clusters

**Key insight I've been missing:** Maybe the attraction state should NOT have the "sees blue" escape, since robots in the black strip naturally see the blue walls nearby and keep leaving.

# ROUND 8/10

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Situation:** Champion stuck at 3045.55 for 4 rounds. Something fundamentally different is needed.

**Deep analysis of what's happening:**
- Score of 3045.55 means on average ~2.5 robots are outside the target per step (3045/1200 ≈ 2.5 robots outside). That's actually quite good - most robots are in the target zone.
- But we need to push this lower. The remaining ~2.5 robots might be stragglers that never find the blue strip, or robots that oscillate in/out.

**Fresh thinking:**
1. The "sees blue → leave attraction" (p=0.5) condition keeps sending robots back to GoToBlue. If they're already on black floor near blue walls, this is counterproductive. **Try removing or reducing this.**
2. Maybe the issue is that robots in the attraction state keep seeing blue (since the blue walls border the black strip) and thus keep re-triggering GoToBlue state unnecessarily.
3. **Critical insight:** The blue walls are RIGHT NEXT to the black floor. So robots on the black floor ALWAYS see blue. With p=0.5, they have 50% chance per step of leaving attraction! This is very bad.
4. **Fix:** Remove the "sees blue" escape OR make it very low probability (p=0.01).
5. Also try: once on black floor, stay with very high attraction and minimal escape.

**Primary hypothesis:** The "sees blue" escape at p=0.5 is the main bug - robots keep leaving the target zone.

# ROUND 9/10

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Situation:** Champion stuck at 3045.55 for 5 consecutive rounds. We're in a deep local optimum. Need radical exploration.

**Root cause analysis:**
- Score 3045.55 / 1200 steps = ~2.5 robots outside per step. So ~17.5/20 robots are in the target zone on average. Good but not great.
- The champion: GoToBlue → black floor detected → Attract. The "sees blue" escape at p=0.5 from attraction IS suspicious.
- But removing it didn't help in previous rounds. So maybe the architecture itself is the limit.

**Radical new ideas:**
1. **Completely different navigation**: Use GoAwayColor(green=2) to push robots toward the blue side instead of GoToColor(blue=3). Green walls are on the opposite side, so going away from green = going toward blue.
2. **Use color 2 (green) avoidance**: `--clr 2` with B=9
3. **Multi-stage approach**: Explore → see blue → go to blue → attract → if gray, explore
4. **Stop state on black floor**: robots that arrive simply stop (minimal disruption)
5. **Try different color IDs**: maybe blue walls are color 4, 5, or 6? Let me try different colors.
6. **Combine both**: GoAwayGreen AND GoToBlue in different states
7. **Try ZigZag for navigation** - might cover ground differently
8. **Use neighbor count to trigger GoToBlue**: if isolated (few neighbors), seek blue

**Key new hypothesis**: Maybe trying clr=4,5,6 for the blue walls could work if color 3 isn't actually blue in this scenario. But the previous champion used clr=3 successfully, so let's keep that.

**Most promising unexplored direction**: GoAwayGreen(B=9, clr=2) as the navigation state instead of GoToBlue.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Final round - need to break out of the 3045.55 local optimum.**

**What we know for certain:**
- The champion uses GoToBlue(3) → black floor detected → Attraction(3.5)
- Multiple escape conditions from attraction state including "sees blue p=0.5" which might be hurting
- Score stuck at 3045.55 for 6 rounds despite many variations

**What hasn't been tried systematically:**
1. The "sees blue p=0.5" escape sends robots away from target. Robots on black floor near blue walls ALWAYS see blue. This means every step they have 50% chance to leave! The 3-condition version (without blue escape) wasn't tested enough.
2. Higher attraction gains (att=5.0) with clean 2-condition escape
3. Completely different color for GoTo - what if the blue walls are actually color 4 (yellow in LED terms but different in camera)?
4. Using GoAwayGreen as primary navigation has never properly been tested
5. **Most important:** Try the exact champion topology but with the "sees blue" escape probability dropped to near 0 (p=0.01) - this could be the key fix

**Final strategy:**
- Keep champion (elitism)
- Several variants without the "sees blue" escape or with very low p
- Try GoAwayGreen(2) navigation
- Try higher attraction gains
- Try completely fresh 3-state with stop on black floor
- Try att=5.0 with no escape conditions at all

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
